[![Map](https://maps.wikimedia.org/img/osm-intl,2,64.833333333333,-147.71666666667,265x200.png?lang=en\&domain=en.wikipedia.org\&title=Alaska\&groups=_359341091129194072cb70317db5a75154bf7499)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:Map/2/64.833333333333/-147.71666666667/en)

Interactive map showing border of Alaska (click to zoom)

**Alaska** ([/əˈlæskə/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/English "Help:IPA/English") [](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/3/34/En-us-Alaska.ogg/En-us-Alaska.ogg.mp3 "Play audio")[ⓘ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:En-us-Alaska.ogg "File:En-us-Alaska.ogg") [*ə-LASS-kə*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Pronunciation_respelling_key "Help:Pronunciation respelling key")) is a [non-contiguous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-contiguous_United_States "Non-contiguous United States") [U.S. state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._state "U.S. state") on the northwest extremity of [North America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_America "North America"). Part of the [Western United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_United_States "Western United States") region, it is one of the two non-contiguous U.S. states, alongside [Hawaii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawaii "Hawaii"). Alaska is also considered to be the northernmost, westernmost, and easternmost (the [Aleutian Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleutian_Islands "Aleutian Islands") cross the [180th meridian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/180th_meridian "180th meridian") into the eastern hemisphere) state in the United States. It borders the [Canadian territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provinces_and_territories_of_Canada "Provinces and territories of Canada") of [Yukon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yukon "Yukon") and the [province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provinces_and_territories_of_Canada "Provinces and territories of Canada") of [British Columbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Columbia "British Columbia") to the east. It shares a western maritime border, in the [Bering Strait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bering_Strait "Bering Strait"), with Russia's [Chukotka Autonomous Okrug](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chukotka_Autonomous_Okrug "Chukotka Autonomous Okrug"). The [Chukchi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chukchi_Sea "Chukchi Sea") and [Beaufort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beaufort_Sea "Beaufort Sea") Seas of the [Arctic Ocean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arctic_Ocean "Arctic Ocean") lie to the north, and the [Pacific Ocean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Ocean "Pacific Ocean") lies to the south. Technically, it is a [semi-exclave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enclave_and_exclave "Enclave and exclave") of the U.S., and is the largest exclave in the world.

Alaska is the [largest U.S. state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_and_territories_by_area "List of U.S. states and territories by area") by area, comprising more total area than the following three largest states of [Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas "Texas"), [California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California "California"), and [Montana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montana "Montana") combined, and is the [seventh-largest subnational division in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_country_subdivisions_by_area "List of country subdivisions by area"). It is the [third-least populous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_and_territories_by_population "List of U.S. states and territories by population") and [most sparsely populated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_and_territories_by_population_density "List of U.S. states and territories by population density") U.S. state; however, with a population of 740,133 as of 2024, it is the most populous territory in North America located mostly north of the [60th parallel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/60th_parallel_north "60th parallel north"), with more than quadruple the combined populations of [Northern Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northern_Canada "Northern Canada") and [Greenland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greenland "Greenland").[\[6\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-2020Census-6) The state contains the four [largest cities in the United States by area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_United_States_cities_by_area "List of United States cities by area"), including the state capital of [Juneau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juneau,_Alaska "Juneau, Alaska"). The state's most populous city is [Anchorage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anchorage,_Alaska "Anchorage, Alaska"), and approximately half of Alaska's residents live within [its metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anchorage_metropolitan_area "Anchorage metropolitan area").

[Indigenous people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_Natives "Alaska Natives") have lived in Alaska for thousands of years, and it is widely believed that the region served as [the entry point for the initial settlement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Settlement_of_the_Americas "Settlement of the Americas") of North America by way of the [Bering land bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bering_land_bridge "Bering land bridge"). The [Russian Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_Empire "Russian Empire") was the first to actively [colonize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_colonization_of_the_Americas "European colonization of the Americas") the area beginning in the 18th century, eventually establishing [Russian America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_America "Russian America"), which spanned most of the current state and promoted and maintained a native [Alaskan Creole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaskan_Creole_people "Alaskan Creole people") population.[\[7\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-lydiablack-7) The expense and logistical difficulty of maintaining this distant possession prompted [its sale to the U.S.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_Purchase "Alaska Purchase") in 1867 for US$7.2 million (equivalent to $157 million in 2023). The area went through several administrative changes before becoming organized as a [territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Territories_of_the_United_States "Territories of the United States") on May 11, 1912. It was admitted as the 49th state of the U.S. on January 3, 1959.[\[8\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-8)

Abundant natural resources have enabled Alaska— with one of the smallest state economies—to have one of the highest [per capita incomes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_by_GDP_per_capita "List of U.S. states by GDP per capita"), with [commercial fishing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commercial_fishing_in_Alaska "Commercial fishing in Alaska"), and the extraction of [natural gas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_gas_in_Alaska "Natural gas in Alaska") and oil, dominating [Alaska's economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Alaska "Economy of Alaska"). U.S. Armed Forces bases and [tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Alaska "Tourism in Alaska") also contribute to the economy; more than half of the state is federally-owned land containing [national forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_National_Forest "United States National Forest"), [national parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_national_parks_of_the_United_States "List of national parks of the United States"), and [wildlife refuges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_wildlife_refuge "National wildlife refuge"). It is among the [most irreligious states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_and_territories_by_religiosity "List of U.S. states and territories by religiosity") and one of the first [to legalize recreational marijuana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Alaska_Measure_2 "2014 Alaska Measure 2"). The Indigenous population of Alaska is proportionally the second highest of any U.S. state, at over 15 percent, after only Hawaii.[\[9\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-9)

## Etymology

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Alaska\&action=edit\&section=1 "Edit section: Etymology")]

The name "Alaska" ([Russian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_language "Russian language"): Аля́ска, [romanized](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization_of_Russian "Romanization of Russian"): *Aljáska*) was introduced during the [Russian colonial period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_America "Russian America") when it was used to refer to the [Alaska Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_Peninsula "Alaska Peninsula"). It was derived from an [Aleut-language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleut_language "Aleut language") [idiom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Idiom "Idiom"), *alaxsxaq*, meaning "the mainland" or, more literally, "the object towards which the action of the sea is directed".[\[10\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Aleut-10)[\[11\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Bright-11)[\[12\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-12)

## History

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Alaska\&action=edit\&section=2 "Edit section: History")]

Main articles: [Prehistory of Alaska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prehistory_of_Alaska "Prehistory of Alaska") and [History of Alaska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Alaska "History of Alaska")

### Pre-colonization

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Alaska\&action=edit\&section=3 "Edit section: Pre-colonization")]

Main article: [Alaska Natives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_Natives "Alaska Natives")

Numerous indigenous peoples occupied Alaska for thousands of years before the arrival of European peoples to the area. Linguistic and DNA studies done here have provided evidence for the settlement of North America by way of the [Bering land bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bering_land_bridge "Bering land bridge").[\[13\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-13)[\[14\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-14) At the [Upward Sun River site](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upward_Sun_River_site "Upward Sun River site") in the [Tanana Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanana_Valley "Tanana Valley") in Alaska, remains of a six-week-old infant were found. The baby's DNA showed that she belonged to a population that was genetically separate from other native groups present elsewhere in the [New World](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_World "New World") at the end of the [Pleistocene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pleistocene "Pleistocene"). Ben Potter, the [University of Alaska Fairbanks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Alaska_Fairbanks "University of Alaska Fairbanks") archaeologist who unearthed the remains at the Upward Sun River site in 2013, named this new group [Ancient Beringian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ancient_Beringian "Ancient Beringian").[\[15\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-15)

The [Tlingit people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tlingit_people "Tlingit people") developed a society with a [matrilineal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matrilineal "Matrilineal") kinship system of property inheritance and descent in what is today Southeast Alaska, along with parts of [British Columbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Columbia "British Columbia") and the [Yukon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yukon "Yukon"). Also in Southeast were the [Haida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haida_people "Haida people"), now well known for their unique arts. The [Tsimshian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsimshian "Tsimshian") people came to Alaska from British Columbia in 1887, when President [Grover Cleveland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grover_Cleveland "Grover Cleveland"), and later the U.S. Congress, granted them permission to settle on [Annette Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annette_Island "Annette Island") and found the town of [Metlakatla, Alaska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metlakatla,_Alaska "Metlakatla, Alaska"). All three of these peoples, as well as other [indigenous peoples of the Pacific Northwest Coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_peoples_of_the_Pacific_Northwest_Coast "Indigenous peoples of the Pacific Northwest Coast"), experienced [smallpox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smallpox "Smallpox") outbreaks from the late 18th through the mid-[19th century](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/19th_century "19th century"), with the most devastating [epidemics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epidemics "Epidemics") occurring in the 1830s and 1860s, resulting in high fatalities and social disruption.[\[16\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-16)

### Colonization

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Alaska\&action=edit\&section=4 "Edit section: Colonization")]

Main articles: [Russian colonization of North America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_colonization_of_North_America "Russian colonization of North America"), [Alaskan Creole people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaskan_Creole_people "Alaskan Creole people"), [Department of Alaska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Department_of_Alaska "Department of Alaska"), [District of Alaska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_of_Alaska "District of Alaska"), [Fairbanks Gold Rush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fairbanks_Gold_Rush "Fairbanks Gold Rush"), [Kobuk River Stampede](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kobuk_River_Stampede "Kobuk River Stampede"), [Nome Gold Rush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nome_Gold_Rush "Nome Gold Rush"), and [Alaska Purchase](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_Purchase "Alaska Purchase")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/49/Russian_Sloop-of-War_Neva.jpg/220px-Russian_Sloop-of-War_Neva.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Russian_Sloop-of-War_Neva.jpg)

The Russian settlement of St. Paul's Harbor (present-day [Kodiak town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kodiak,_Alaska "Kodiak, Alaska")), [Kodiak Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kodiak_Island "Kodiak Island"), 1814

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/40/Miners_climb_Chilkoot.jpg/220px-Miners_climb_Chilkoot.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Miners_climb_Chilkoot.jpg)

Miners and prospectors climb the [Chilkoot Trail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chilkoot_Trail "Chilkoot Trail") during the 1898 [Klondike Gold Rush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Klondike_Gold_Rush "Klondike Gold Rush").

Some researchers believe the first Russian settlement in Alaska was established in the 17th century.[\[17\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-17) According to this hypothesis, in 1648 several [koches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koch_\(boat\) "Koch (boat)") of [Semyon Dezhnyov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Semyon_Dezhnyov "Semyon Dezhnyov")'s expedition came ashore in Alaska by storm and founded this settlement. This hypothesis is based on the testimony of [Chukchi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chukchi_people "Chukchi people") geographer Nikolai Daurkin, who had visited Alaska in 1764–1765 and who had reported on a village on the Kheuveren River, populated by "bearded men" who "pray to the [icons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Icons "Icons")". Some modern researchers associate Kheuveren with [Koyuk River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koyuk_River "Koyuk River").[\[18\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-18)

The first European vessel to reach Alaska is generally held to be the *St. Gabriel* under the authority of the surveyor [M. S. Gvozdev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikhail_Gvozdev "Mikhail Gvozdev") and assistant navigator [I. Fyodorov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivan_Fyodorov_\(navigator\) "Ivan Fyodorov (navigator)") on August 21, 1732, during an expedition of Siberian Cossack A. F. Shestakov and Russian explorer [Dmitry Pavlutsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dmitry_Pavlutsky "Dmitry Pavlutsky") (1729–1735).[\[19\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-19) Another European contact with Alaska occurred in 1741, when [Vitus Bering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vitus_Bering "Vitus Bering") led an [expedition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Kamchatka_expedition "Second Kamchatka expedition") for the Russian Navy aboard the *St. Peter*. After his crew returned to Russia with [sea otter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sea_otter "Sea otter") pelts judged to be the finest fur in the world, small associations of [fur traders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fur_trade "Fur trade") began to sail from the shores of Siberia toward the Aleutian Islands. The first permanent European settlement was founded in 1784.

Between 1774 and 1800, [Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viceroyalty_of_New_Spain "Viceroyalty of New Spain") sent several [expeditions to Alaska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_expeditions_to_the_Pacific_Northwest "Spanish expeditions to the Pacific Northwest") to assert its claim over the Pacific Northwest. In 1789, a Spanish settlement and [fort](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_San_Miguel "Fort San Miguel") were built in [Nootka Sound](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nootka_Sound "Nootka Sound"). These expeditions gave names to places such as [Valdez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valdez,_Alaska "Valdez, Alaska"), [Bucareli Sound](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bucareli_Sound "Bucareli Sound"), and [Cordova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cordova,_Alaska "Cordova, Alaska"). Later, the [Russian-American Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian-American_Company "Russian-American Company") carried out an expanded colonization program during the early-to-mid-19th century. [Sitka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sitka,_Alaska "Sitka, Alaska"), renamed [New Archangel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Archangel "New Archangel") from 1804 to 1867, on [Baranof Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baranof_Island "Baranof Island") in the [Alexander Archipelago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Archipelago "Alexander Archipelago") in what is now [Southeast Alaska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southeast_Alaska "Southeast Alaska"), became the capital of [Russian America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_America "Russian America"). It remained the capital after the colony was transferred to the United States. The Russians never fully colonized Alaska, and the colony was never very profitable. Evidence of Russian settlement in names and churches survives throughout southeastern Alaska.[\[20\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-20)

In 1867, [William H. Seward](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_H._Seward "William H. Seward"), the [United States Secretary of State](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Secretary_of_State "United States Secretary of State") under President [Andrew Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Johnson "Andrew Johnson"), negotiated the [Alaska Purchase](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_Purchase "Alaska Purchase") (referred to pejoratively as Seward's Folly) with the Russians for $7.2 million.[\[21\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-21) Russia's contemporary ruler [Tsar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsar "Tsar") [Alexander II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_II_of_Russia "Alexander II of Russia"), the [Emperor of the Russian Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emperor_of_the_Russian_Empire "Emperor of the Russian Empire"), [King of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_of_Poland "King of Poland") and [Grand Duke of Finland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grand_Duke_of_Finland "Grand Duke of Finland"), also planned the sale;[\[22\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-22) the purchase was made on March 30, 1867. Six months later the commissioners arrived in Sitka and the formal transfer was arranged; the formal flag-raising took place at Fort Sitka on October 18, 1867. In the ceremony, 250 uniformed U.S. soldiers marched to the governor's house at "Castle Hill", where the Russian troops lowered the Russian flag and the U.S. flag was raised. This event is celebrated as [Alaska Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_Day "Alaska Day"), a legal holiday on October 18.

Alaska was loosely governed by the military initially and was administered as a [district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_of_Alaska "District of Alaska") starting in 1884, with a governor appointed by the United States president. A federal [district court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_territorial_court "United States territorial court") was headquartered in Sitka. For most of Alaska's first decade under the United States flag, Sitka was the only community inhabited by American settlers. They organized a "provisional city government", which was Alaska's first municipal government, but not in a legal sense.[\[23\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-23) Legislation allowing Alaskan communities to legally [incorporate as cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipal_corporation "Municipal corporation") did not come about until 1900, and [home rule](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Home_rule#Home_rule_in_the_United_States "Home rule") for cities was extremely limited or unavailable until statehood took effect in 1959.

### U.S. territorial incorporation

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Alaska\&action=edit\&section=5 "Edit section: U.S. territorial incorporation")]

Main articles: [Organic act § List of organic acts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organic_act#List_of_organic_acts "Organic act"), and [Territory of Alaska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Territory_of_Alaska "Territory of Alaska")

Starting in the 1890s and stretching in some places to the early 1910s, gold rushes in Alaska and the nearby Yukon Territory brought thousands of miners and settlers to Alaska. From 1879 to 1920, Alaska produced a cumulative total of over $460,000,000 ($6,691,927,500 inflation-adjusted) of mineral production.[\[24\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-24) Alaska was officially incorporated as an organized territory in 1912. Alaska's capital, which had been in Sitka until 1906, was moved north to [Juneau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juneau,_Alaska "Juneau, Alaska"). Construction of the [Alaska Governor's Mansion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_Governor%27s_Mansion "Alaska Governor's Mansion") began that same year. European immigrants from Norway and Sweden also settled in southeast Alaska, where they entered the fishing and logging industries.

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a8/US_troops_at_the_Battle_of_Attu.jpg/220px-US_troops_at_the_Battle_of_Attu.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:US_troops_at_the_Battle_of_Attu.jpg)

U.S. troops navigate snow and ice during the [Battle of Attu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Attu "Battle of Attu") in May 1943.

During [World War II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_War_II "World War II"), the [Aleutian Islands Campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleutian_Islands_Campaign "Aleutian Islands Campaign") focused on [Attu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attu_Island "Attu Island"), [Agattu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agattu "Agattu") and [Kiska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiska_Island "Kiska Island"), all of which were occupied by the [Empire of Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_of_Japan "Empire of Japan").[\[a\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-25) During the Japanese occupation, an American civilian and two [United States Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Navy "United States Navy") personnel were killed at Attu and Kiska respectively, and nearly a total of 50 Aleut civilians and eight sailors were interned in Japan. About half of the Aleuts died during the period of internment.[\[25\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-26) [Unalaska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unalaska "Unalaska")/[Dutch Harbor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dutch_Harbor "Dutch Harbor") and [Adak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adak,_Alaska "Adak, Alaska") became significant bases for the [United States Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Army "United States Army"), [United States Army Air Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Army_Air_Forces "United States Army Air Forces") and United States Navy. The United States [Lend-Lease](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lend-Lease "Lend-Lease") program involved flying American warplanes through Canada to [Fairbanks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fairbanks,_Alaska "Fairbanks, Alaska") and then [Nome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nome,_Alaska "Nome, Alaska"); Soviet pilots took possession of these aircraft, ferrying them to fight the German invasion of the [Soviet Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soviet_Union "Soviet Union"). The construction of military bases contributed to the population growth of some Alaskan cities.

### Statehood

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Alaska\&action=edit\&section=6 "Edit section: Statehood")]

See also: [Alaska Statehood Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_Statehood_Act "Alaska Statehood Act"), [Admission to the Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Admission_to_the_Union "Admission to the Union"), and [List of U.S. states by date of admission to the Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._states_by_date_of_admission_to_the_Union "List of U.S. states by date of admission to the Union")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/17/Alaskan_Senators_with_49_Star_Flag.jpg/170px-Alaskan_Senators_with_49_Star_Flag.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Alaskan_Senators_with_49_Star_Flag.jpg)

[Bob Bartlett](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Bartlett "Bob Bartlett") and [Ernest Gruening](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ernest_Gruening "Ernest Gruening"), Alaska's inaugural U.S. Senators, hold the 49 star U.S. Flag after the admission of Alaska as the 49th state.

Statehood for Alaska was an important cause of [James Wickersham](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Wickersham "James Wickersham") early in his tenure as a congressional delegate.[\[26\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-27) Decades later, the statehood movement gained its first real momentum following a territorial referendum in 1946. The Alaska Statehood Committee and Alaska's Constitutional Convention would soon follow. Statehood supporters also found themselves fighting major battles against political foes, mostly in the U.S. Congress but also within Alaska. Statehood was approved by the U.S. Congress on July 7, 1958; Alaska was officially proclaimed a state on January 3, 1959.[\[27\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-28)

### Good Friday earthquake

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Alaska\&action=edit\&section=7 "Edit section: Good Friday earthquake")]

Main article: [1964 Alaska earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1964_Alaska_earthquake "1964 Alaska earthquake")

On March 27, 1964, the massive [Good Friday earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1964_Alaska_earthquake "1964 Alaska earthquake") killed 133 people and destroyed several villages and portions of large coastal communities, mainly by the resultant [tsunamis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsunamis "Tsunamis") and landslides. It was the [fourth-most-powerful earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Largest_earthquakes_by_magnitude "Largest earthquakes by magnitude") in recorded history, with a [moment magnitude](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moment_magnitude_scale "Moment magnitude scale") of 9.2 (more than a thousand times as powerful as the [1989 San Francisco earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1989_Loma_Prieta_earthquake "1989 Loma Prieta earthquake")).[\[28\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-29) The time of day (5:36 pm), time of year (spring) and location of the [epicenter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epicenter "Epicenter") were all cited as factors in potentially sparing thousands of lives, particularly in Anchorage. Alaska suffered a more severe [megathrust earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Megathrust_earthquake "Megathrust earthquake") on July 11, 1585, estimated at magnitude 9.25, which remains the most powerful earthquake recorded in [North American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_America "North America") history, and the second [most powerful earthquake recorded](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lists_of_earthquakes#Largest_earthquakes_by_magnitude "Lists of earthquakes") in world history.[\[29\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-30)

The Good Friday earthquake lasted 4 minutes and 38 seconds. Six hundred miles (970 km) of fault ruptured at once and moved up to 60 ft (18 m), releasing about 500 years of stress buildup. [Soil liquefaction](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soil_liquefaction "Soil liquefaction"), fissures, landslides, and other ground failures caused major structural damage in several communities and much damage to property. [Anchorage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anchorage,_Alaska "Anchorage, Alaska") sustained great destruction or damage to many inadequately [earthquake-engineered](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Earthquake_engineering "Earthquake engineering") houses, buildings, and infrastructure (paved streets, sidewalks, water and sewer mains, electrical systems, and other human-made equipment), particularly in the several landslide zones along [Knik Arm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knik_Arm "Knik Arm"). Two hundred miles (320 km) southwest, some areas near [Kodiak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kodiak_Island "Kodiak Island") were permanently raised by 30 feet (9 m). Southeast of Anchorage, areas around the head of [Turnagain Arm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turnagain_Arm "Turnagain Arm") near [Girdwood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Girdwood,_Anchorage "Girdwood, Anchorage") and [Portage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portage_\(Anchorage\) "Portage (Anchorage)") dropped as much as 8 feet (2.4 m), requiring reconstruction and fill to raise the [Seward Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seward_Highway "Seward Highway") above the new high [tide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tide "Tide") mark.[\[30\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-31)

In [Prince William Sound](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_William_Sound "Prince William Sound"), [Port Valdez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Valdez "Port of Valdez") suffered a massive underwater landslide, resulting in the deaths of 32 people between the collapse of the [Valdez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valdez,_Alaska "Valdez, Alaska") city harbor and docks, and inside the ship that was docked there at the time. Nearby, a 27-foot (8.2 m) tsunami destroyed the village of [Chenega](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chenega,_Alaska "Chenega, Alaska"), killing 23 of the 68 people who lived there; survivors out-ran the wave, climbing to high ground. Post-quake tsunamis severely affected [Whittier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Whittier,_Alaska "Whittier, Alaska"), [Seward](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seward,_Alaska "Seward, Alaska"), Kodiak, and other Alaskan communities, as well as people and property in British Columbia, [Washington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_\(state\) "Washington (state)"), [Oregon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oregon "Oregon"), and [California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California "California").[\[31\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-32) Tsunamis also caused damage in [Hawaii](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hawaii "Hawaii") and [Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japan "Japan"). Evidence of motion directly related to the earthquake was also reported from [Florida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Florida "Florida") and [Texas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas "Texas").

Alaska had never experienced a major disaster in a highly populated area before and had very limited resources for dealing with the effects of such an event. In Anchorage, at the urging of geologist [Lidia Selkregg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lidia_Selkregg "Lidia Selkregg"), the City of Anchorage and the Alaska State Housing Authority appointed a team of 40 scientists, including geologists, soil scientists, and engineers, to assess the damage done by the earthquake to the city.[\[32\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-:0a-33) The team, called the Engineering and Geological Evaluation Group, was headed by [Ruth A. M. Schmidt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruth_A._M._Schmidt "Ruth A. M. Schmidt"), a geology professor at the [University of Alaska Anchorage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Alaska_Anchorage "University of Alaska Anchorage"). The team of scientists came into conflict with local developers and downtown business owners who wanted to immediately rebuild; the scientists wanted to identify future dangers to ensure that the rebuilt infrastructure would be safe.[\[33\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-34) The team produced a report on May 8, 1964, just a little more than a month after the earthquake.[\[32\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-:0a-33)[\[34\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-35)

The United States military, which has a large active presence in Alaska, also stepped in to assist within moments of the end of the quake. The U.S. Army rapidly re-established communications with the lower 48 states, deployed troops to assist the citizens of Anchorage, and dispatched a convoy to Valdez.[\[35\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Hand-36) On the advice of military and civilian leaders, President [Lyndon B. Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lyndon_B._Johnson "Lyndon B. Johnson") declared all of Alaska a major disaster area the day after the quake. The U.S. Navy and [U.S. Coast Guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Coast_Guard "United States Coast Guard") deployed ships to isolated coastal communities to assist with immediate needs. Bad weather and poor visibility hampered air rescue and observation efforts the day after the quake, but on Sunday the 29th the situation improved and rescue helicopters and observation aircraft were deployed.[\[35\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Hand-36) A military airlift immediately began shipping relief supplies to Alaska, eventually delivering 2,570,000 pounds (1,170,000 kg) of food and other supplies.[\[36\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Galvin-37) Broadcast journalist, [Genie Chance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genie_Chance "Genie Chance"), assisted in recovery and relief efforts, staying on the [KENI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KENI "KENI") air waves over Anchorage for more than 24 continuous hours as the voice of calm from her temporary post within the Anchorage Public Safety Building.[\[37\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-:2-38) She was effectively designated as the public safety officer by the city's police chief.[\[37\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-:2-38) Chance provided breaking news of the catastrophic events that continued to develop following the magnitude 9.2 earthquake, and she served as the voice of the public safety office, coordinating response efforts, connecting available resources to needs around the community, disseminating information about shelters and prepared food rations, passing messages of well-being between loved ones, and helping to reunite families.[\[38\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-39)

In the longer term, the [U.S. Army Corps of Engineers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Army_Corps_of_Engineers "U.S. Army Corps of Engineers") led the effort to rebuild roads, clear debris, and establish new townsites for communities that had been completely destroyed, at a cost of $110 million.[\[36\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Galvin-37) The [West Coast and Alaska Tsunami Warning Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Coast_and_Alaska_Tsunami_Warning_Center "West Coast and Alaska Tsunami Warning Center") was formed as a direct response to the disaster. Federal disaster relief funds paid for reconstruction as well as financially supporting the devastated infrastructure of Alaska's government, spending hundreds of millions of dollars that helped keep Alaska financially solvent until the discovery of massive oil deposits at [Prudhoe Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prudhoe_Bay "Prudhoe Bay"). At the order of the [U.S. Defense Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Department_of_Defense "United States Department of Defense"), the [Alaska National Guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_National_Guard "Alaska National Guard") founded the Alaska Division of Emergency Services to respond to any future disasters.[\[35\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-Hand-36)

### Oil boom

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Alaska\&action=edit\&section=8 "Edit section: Oil boom")]

The 1968 discovery of oil at Prudhoe Bay and the 1977 completion of the [Trans-Alaska Pipeline System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trans-Alaska_Pipeline_System "Trans-Alaska Pipeline System") led to an oil boom. Royalty revenues from oil have funded large state budgets from 1980 onward.

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9e/OilPoolFromValdezSpill.jpeg/220px-OilPoolFromValdezSpill.jpeg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:OilPoolFromValdezSpill.jpeg)

Oil pooled on rocks on the shore of Prince William Sound after the 1989 Exxon Valdez oil spill.

Oil production was not the only economic value of Alaska's land. In the second half of the 20th century, Alaska discovered [tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism "Tourism") as an important source of revenue. Tourism became popular after World War II when military personnel stationed in the region returned home praising its natural splendor. The [Alcan Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_Highway "Alaska Highway"), built during the war, and the [Alaska Marine Highway System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_Marine_Highway "Alaska Marine Highway"), completed in 1963, made the state more accessible than before. Tourism has become increasingly important in Alaska, and today over 1.4 million people visit the state each year.[\[39\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-40)

With tourism more vital to the economy, [environmentalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmentalism "Environmentalism") also rose in importance. The [Alaska National Interest Lands Conservation Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_National_Interest_Lands_Conservation_Act "Alaska National Interest Lands Conservation Act") (ANILCA) of 1980 added 53.7 million acres (217,000 km2) to the [National Wildlife Refuge system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Wildlife_Refuge "National Wildlife Refuge"), parts of 25 rivers to the [National Wild and Scenic Rivers system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Wild_and_Scenic_Rivers_System "National Wild and Scenic Rivers System"), 3.3 million acres (13,000 km2) to [National Forest lands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_forest_\(United_States\) "National forest (United States)"), and 43.6 million acres (176,000 km2) to [National Park land](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Park_Service "National Park Service"). Because of the Act, Alaska now contains two-thirds of all American national parklands. Today, more than half of Alaskan land is owned by the [Federal Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_government_of_the_United_States "Federal government of the United States").[\[40\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-41)

In 1989, the *[Exxon Valdez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exxon_Valdez "Exxon Valdez")* hit a reef in the Prince William Sound, [spilling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Exxon_Valdez_oil_spill "Exxon Valdez oil spill") more than 11 million gallons (42 megalitres) of crude oil over 1,100 miles (1,800 km) of coastline. Today, the battle between philosophies of development and conservation is seen in the contentious debate over oil drilling in the [Arctic National Wildlife Refuge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arctic_National_Wildlife_Refuge "Arctic National Wildlife Refuge") and the proposed [Pebble Mine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pebble_Mine "Pebble Mine").[\[41\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-42)

## Geography

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Alaska\&action=edit\&section=9 "Edit section: Geography")]

Main article: [Geography of Alaska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geography_of_Alaska "Geography of Alaska")

Located at the northwest corner of [North America](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_America "North America"), Alaska is the northernmost and westernmost state in the United States, but also has the most easterly longitude in the United States because the [Aleutian Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleutian_Islands "Aleutian Islands") extend into the [Eastern Hemisphere](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Hemisphere "Eastern Hemisphere").[\[42\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-43) Alaska is the only non-[contiguous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contiguous_United_States "Contiguous United States") U.S. state on continental North America; about 500 miles (800 km) of Canadian territory consisting of [British Columbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Columbia "British Columbia") (in [Canada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canada "Canada")) separates Alaska from [Washington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washington_\(state\) "Washington (state)"). It is technically part of the [continental U.S.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continental_United_States "Continental United States"), but is not usually included in the colloquial use of the term; Alaska is not part of the [contiguous U.S.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contiguous_United_States "Contiguous United States"), often called "[the Lower 48](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Outside_\(Alaska\) "Outside (Alaska)")". The capital city, [Juneau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juneau,_Alaska "Juneau, Alaska"), is situated on the mainland of the North American continent but is not connected by road to the rest of the North American highway system. The largest lake in Alaska is [Lake Illiamna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iliamna_Lake "Iliamna Lake").

The state is bordered by Canada's [Yukon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yukon "Yukon") and [British Columbia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Columbia "British Columbia") to the east (making it the only state to border only a [Canadian territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Provinces_and_territories_of_Canada "Provinces and territories of Canada")); the [Gulf of Alaska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gulf_of_Alaska "Gulf of Alaska") and the Pacific Ocean to the south and southwest; the [Bering Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bering_Sea "Bering Sea"), [Bering Strait](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bering_Strait "Bering Strait"), and [Chukchi Sea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chukchi_Sea "Chukchi Sea") to the west; and the Arctic Ocean to the north. Alaska's territorial waters touch Russia's territorial waters in the Bering Strait, as the Russian [Big Diomede Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Diomede_Island "Big Diomede Island") and Alaskan [Little Diomede Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_Diomede_Island "Little Diomede Island") are only 3 miles (4.8 km) apart. Alaska has a longer coastline than all the other U.S. states combined.[\[43\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-44)

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/59/Alaska_area_compared_to_conterminous_US.svg/220px-Alaska_area_compared_to_conterminous_US.svg.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Alaska_area_compared_to_conterminous_US.svg)

Alaska's size compared with the [48 contiguous states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contiguous_United_States "Contiguous United States") ([Albers equal-area conic projection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albers_projection "Albers projection"))

At 663,268 square miles (1,717,856 km2) in total area, Alaska is by far the largest state in the United States. Alaska is more than twice the size of the second-largest U.S. state (Texas), and it is larger than the next three largest states (Texas, California, and Montana) combined. Alaska is the seventh [largest subnational division in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_the_largest_country_subdivisions_by_area "List of the largest country subdivisions by area"). If it was an independent nation, it would be the 18th largest country in the world; almost the same size as [Iran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran "Iran").[\[44\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-45)

With its myriad islands, Alaska has nearly 34,000 miles (55,000 km) of tidal shoreline. The Aleutian Islands chain extends west from the southern tip of the [Alaska Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_Peninsula "Alaska Peninsula"). Many active [volcanoes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volcano "Volcano") are found in the Aleutians and in coastal regions. [Unimak Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unimak_Island "Unimak Island"), for example, is home to [Mount Shishaldin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Shishaldin "Mount Shishaldin"), which is an occasionally smoldering volcano that rises to 10,000 feet (3,000 m) above the North Pacific. The chain of volcanoes extends to [Mount Spurr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Spurr "Mount Spurr"), west of Anchorage on the mainland. Geologists have identified Alaska as part of [Wrangellia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wrangellia "Wrangellia"), a large region consisting of multiple states and Canadian provinces in the [Pacific Northwest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Northwest "Pacific Northwest"), which is actively undergoing [continent building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plate_tectonics "Plate tectonics").[\[45\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-46)

One of the world's largest tides occurs in [Turnagain Arm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turnagain_Arm "Turnagain Arm"), just south of Anchorage, where tidal differences can be more than 35 feet (10.7 m).[\[46\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-47)

Alaska has more than 409,000 natural [lakes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lake "Lake") at least one [hectare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hectare "Hectare") or bigger.[\[47\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-48) [Marshlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marshland "Marshland") and wetland [permafrost](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Permafrost "Permafrost") cover 188,320 square miles (487,700 km2) (mostly in northern, western and southwest flatlands). Glacier ice covers about 28,957 square miles (75,000 km2) of Alaska.[\[48\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-49) The [Bering Glacier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bering_Glacier "Bering Glacier") is the largest glacier in North America, covering 2,008 square miles (5,200 km2) alone.[\[49\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-50)

### Regions

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Alaska\&action=edit\&section=10 "Edit section: Regions")]

There are no officially defined borders demarcating the various regions of Alaska, however there are five/six regions that the state is most commonly broken up into:

#### South Central

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Alaska\&action=edit\&section=11 "Edit section: South Central")]

Main article: [South Central Alaska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Central_Alaska "South Central Alaska")

The most populous region of Alaska contains [Anchorage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anchorage "Anchorage"), the [Matanuska-Susitna Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matanuska-Susitna_Valley "Matanuska-Susitna Valley") and the [Kenai Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kenai_Peninsula "Kenai Peninsula"). Rural, mostly unpopulated areas south of the [Alaska Range](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_Range "Alaska Range") and west of the [Wrangell Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wrangell_Mountains "Wrangell Mountains") also fall within the definition of South Central, as do the [Prince William Sound](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prince_William_Sound "Prince William Sound") area and the communities of [Cordova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cordova,_Alaska "Cordova, Alaska") and [Valdez](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valdez,_Alaska "Valdez, Alaska").[\[50\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-cities-51)

#### Southeast

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Alaska\&action=edit\&section=12 "Edit section: Southeast")]

Main article: [Southeast Alaska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southeast_Alaska "Southeast Alaska")

Also referred to as the Panhandle or [Inside Passage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inside_Passage "Inside Passage"), this is the region of Alaska closest to the contiguous states. As such, this was where most of the initial non-indigenous settlement occurred in the years following the [Alaska Purchase](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_Purchase "Alaska Purchase"). The region is dominated by the [Alexander Archipelago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alexander_Archipelago "Alexander Archipelago") as well as the [Tongass National Forest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tongass_National_Forest "Tongass National Forest"), the largest national forest in the United States. It contains the state capital [Juneau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juneau,_Alaska "Juneau, Alaska"), the former capital [Sitka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sitka,_Alaska "Sitka, Alaska"), and [Ketchikan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ketchikan "Ketchikan"), at one time Alaska's largest city.[\[51\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-52) The [Alaska Marine Highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_Marine_Highway "Alaska Marine Highway") provides a vital surface transportation link throughout the area and country, as only three communities ([Haines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haines,_Alaska "Haines, Alaska"), [Hyder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyder,_Alaska "Hyder, Alaska") and [Skagway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skagway,_Alaska "Skagway, Alaska")) enjoy direct connections to the contiguous North American road system.[\[52\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-DOT_Document-53)

#### Interior

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Alaska\&action=edit\&section=13 "Edit section: Interior")]

Main article: [Alaska Interior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_Interior "Alaska Interior")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8b/Denali_Mt_McKinley.jpg/220px-Denali_Mt_McKinley.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Denali_Mt_McKinley.jpg)

[Denali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denali "Denali") is the highest peak in North America

The Interior is the largest region of Alaska; much of it is uninhabited wilderness. [Fairbanks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fairbanks,_Alaska "Fairbanks, Alaska") is the only large city in the region. [Denali National Park and Preserve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denali_National_Park_and_Preserve "Denali National Park and Preserve") is located here. [Denali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denali "Denali"), formerly Mount McKinley, is the highest mountain in North America and is also located here.

#### North Slope

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Alaska\&action=edit\&section=14 "Edit section: North Slope")]

Main article: [Alaska North Slope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_North_Slope "Alaska North Slope")

The North Slope is mostly [tundra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tundra "Tundra") peppered with small villages. The area is known for its massive reserves of crude oil and contains both the [National Petroleum Reserve–Alaska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Petroleum_Reserve%E2%80%93Alaska "National Petroleum Reserve–Alaska") and the [Prudhoe Bay Oil Field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prudhoe_Bay_Oil_Field "Prudhoe Bay Oil Field").[\[53\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-54) The city of [Utqiaġvik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Utqia%C4%A1vik,_Alaska "Utqiaġvik, Alaska"), formerly known as Barrow, is the northernmost city in the United States and is located here. The [Northwest Arctic area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northwest_Arctic_Borough,_Alaska "Northwest Arctic Borough, Alaska"), anchored by [Kotzebue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kotzebue,_Alaska "Kotzebue, Alaska") and also containing the [Kobuk River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kobuk_River "Kobuk River") valley, is often considered part of this region. The respective [Inupiat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inupiat_people "Inupiat people") of the North Slope and of the Northwest Arctic seldom consider themselves to be one people.[\[54\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-55)

#### Southwest

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Alaska\&action=edit\&section=15 "Edit section: Southwest")]

Main article: [Southwest Alaska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southwest_Alaska "Southwest Alaska")

Southwest Alaska is a sparsely inhabited region stretching some 500 miles (800 km) inland from the Bering Sea. Most of the population lives along the coast. [Kodiak Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kodiak_Island "Kodiak Island") is also located in the Southwest. The massive [Yukon–Kuskokwim Delta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yukon%E2%80%93Kuskokwim_Delta "Yukon–Kuskokwim Delta"), one of the largest river deltas in the world, is here. Portions of the [Alaska Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_Peninsula "Alaska Peninsula") are considered part of the Southwest, with the Aleutian Islands often (but not always) being grouped in as well.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

#### Aleutian Islands

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Alaska\&action=edit\&section=16 "Edit section: Aleutian Islands")]

Main article: [Aleutian Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aleutian_Islands "Aleutian Islands")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/63/Aleutian_Islands_with_180th_meridian_and_International_Date_Line_%28cropped%29.png/220px-Aleutian_Islands_with_180th_meridian_and_International_Date_Line_%28cropped%29.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Aleutian_Islands_with_180th_meridian_and_International_Date_Line_\(cropped\).png)

Although entirely east of the [International Date Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Date_Line "International Date Line") (the triangular kink in the line was agreed upon the [U.S. acquisition of Alaska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_Purchase "Alaska Purchase")), the Aleutian Islands cross the [180th meridian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/180th_meridian "180th meridian"), such that they contain both the westernmost ([Amatignak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amatignak_Island "Amatignak Island")) and the easternmost ([Semisopochnoi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Semisopochnoi_Island "Semisopochnoi Island").) points in the United States.

While primarily part of Southwest Alaska when grouped economically, the Aleutian islands are sometimes recognized as an alternate group from the rest of the region due to the geographic separation from the continent. More than 300 small volcanic islands make up this chain, which stretches more than 1,200 miles (1,900 km) into the Pacific Ocean. Some of these islands fall in the Eastern Hemisphere, but the [International Date Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Date_Line "International Date Line") was drawn west of [180°](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/180th_meridian "180th meridian") to keep the whole state, and thus the entire North American continent, within the same legal day. Two of the islands, [Attu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attu_Island "Attu Island") and [Kiska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kiska "Kiska"), were occupied by Japanese forces during World War II.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]

### Land ownership

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Alaska\&action=edit\&section=17 "Edit section: Land ownership")]

|                                                                                                                                                                            |                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| [![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/50px-Question_book-new.svg.png)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg) | This section **needs additional citations for [verification](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability "Wikipedia:Verifiability")**. Please help [improve this article](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:EditPage/Alaska "Special:EditPage/Alaska") by [adding citations to reliable sources](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners "Help:Referencing for beginners") in this section. Unsourced material may be challenged and removed. *Find sources:* ["Alaska"](https://www.google.com/search?as_eq=wikipedia\&q=%22Alaska%22) – [news](https://www.google.com/search?tbm=nws\&q=%22Alaska%22+-wikipedia\&tbs=ar:1) **·** [newspapers](https://www.google.com/search?\&q=%22Alaska%22\&tbs=bkt:s\&tbm=bks) **·** [books](https://www.google.com/search?tbs=bks:1\&q=%22Alaska%22+-wikipedia) **·** [scholar](https://scholar.google.com/scholar?q=%22Alaska%22) **·** [JSTOR](https://www.jstor.org/action/doBasicSearch?Query=%22Alaska%22\&acc=on\&wc=on) *(January 2025)**&#x20;([Learn how and when to remove this message](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal "Help:Maintenance template removal"))* |

According to an October 1998 report by the [United States Bureau of Land Management](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Bureau_of_Land_Management "United States Bureau of Land Management"), approximately 65% of Alaska is owned and managed by the [U.S. federal government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federal_government_of_the_United_States "Federal government of the United States") as public lands, including a multitude of [national forests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_National_Forest "United States National Forest"), national parks, and [national wildlife refuges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_wildlife_refuge "National wildlife refuge").[\[55\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-56) Of these, the [Bureau of Land Management](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bureau_of_Land_Management "Bureau of Land Management") manages 87 million acres (35 million hectares), or 23.8% of the state. The [Arctic National Wildlife Refuge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arctic_National_Wildlife_Refuge "Arctic National Wildlife Refuge") is managed by the [United States Fish and Wildlife Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Fish_and_Wildlife_Service "United States Fish and Wildlife Service"). It is the world's largest wildlife refuge, comprising 16 million acres (6.5 million hectares).

Of the remaining land area, the state of Alaska owns 101 million acres (41 million hectares), its entitlement under the [Alaska Statehood Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_Statehood_Act "Alaska Statehood Act"). A portion of that acreage is occasionally ceded to the organized boroughs presented above, under the statutory provisions pertaining to newly formed boroughs. Smaller portions are set aside for rural subdivisions and other homesteading-related opportunities. These are not very popular due to the often remote and roadless locations. The [University of Alaska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Alaska "University of Alaska"), as a [land grant university](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Land_grant_university "Land grant university"), also owns substantial acreage which it manages independently.

Another 44 million acres (18 million hectares) are owned by 12 regional, and scores of local, Native corporations created under the [Alaska Native Claims Settlement Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_Native_Claims_Settlement_Act "Alaska Native Claims Settlement Act") (ANCSA) of 1971. [Regional Native corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_Native_Regional_Corporation "Alaska Native Regional Corporation") [Doyon, Limited](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doyon,_Limited "Doyon, Limited") often promotes itself as the largest private landowner in Alaska in advertisements and other communications. Provisions of ANCSA allowing the corporations' land holdings to be sold on the open market starting in 1991 were repealed before they could take effect. Effectively, the corporations hold title (including subsurface title in many cases, a privilege denied to individual Alaskans) but cannot sell the land. [Individual Native allotments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_Native_Allotment_Act "Alaska Native Allotment Act") are sold on the open market.

Various private interests own the remaining land, totaling about one percent of the state. Alaska is, by a large margin, the state with the smallest percentage of private land ownership when Native corporation holdings are excluded.

### Alaska Heritage Resources Survey

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Alaska\&action=edit\&section=18 "Edit section: Alaska Heritage Resources Survey")]

The Alaska Heritage Resources Survey (AHRS) is a restricted [inventory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inventory "Inventory") of all reported [historic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historic_site "Historic site") and [prehistoric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prehistoric "Prehistoric") sites within the U.S. state of Alaska; it is maintained by the Office of History and Archaeology. The survey's inventory of cultural resources includes objects, structures, buildings, sites, districts, and travel ways, with a general provision that they are more than fifty years old. As of 31 January 2012[\[update\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Alaska\&action=edit), more than 35,000 sites have been reported.[\[56\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-57)

### Cities, towns and boroughs

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Alaska\&action=edit\&section=19 "Edit section: Cities, towns and boroughs")]

Further information: [List of cities in Alaska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_in_Alaska "List of cities in Alaska") and [List of boroughs and census areas in Alaska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_boroughs_and_census_areas_in_Alaska "List of boroughs and census areas in Alaska")

See also: [List of Alaska locations by per capita income](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Alaska_locations_by_per_capita_income "List of Alaska locations by per capita income")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/26/Anchorage1.jpg/180px-Anchorage1.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Anchorage1.jpg)

[Anchorage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anchorage,_Alaska "Anchorage, Alaska"), Alaska's largest city

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9f/Aerial_view_of_Fairbanks_Alaska_skyline_%28Quintin_Soloviev%29.jpg/180px-Aerial_view_of_Fairbanks_Alaska_skyline_%28Quintin_Soloviev%29.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Aerial_view_of_Fairbanks_Alaska_skyline_\(Quintin_Soloviev\).jpg)

[Fairbanks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fairbanks,_Alaska "Fairbanks, Alaska"), Alaska's second-largest city and by a significant margin the largest city in [Alaska's interior](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_Interior "Alaska Interior")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/14/Downtown_Juneau_with_Mount_Juneau_rising_in_the_background.jpg/180px-Downtown_Juneau_with_Mount_Juneau_rising_in_the_background.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Downtown_Juneau_with_Mount_Juneau_rising_in_the_background.jpg)

[Juneau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juneau,_Alaska "Juneau, Alaska"), Alaska's third-largest city and [its capital](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_capitals_in_the_United_States "List of capitals in the United States")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f0/Bethel_Alaska_aerial_view.jpg/180px-Bethel_Alaska_aerial_view.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Bethel_Alaska_aerial_view.jpg)

[Bethel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bethel,_Alaska "Bethel, Alaska"), the largest city in the [Unorganized Borough](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unorganized_Borough "Unorganized Borough") and in [rural Alaska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Bush_\(Alaska\) "The Bush (Alaska)")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/76/Looking_into_the_sun_from_over_downtown_onto_the_Homer_Spit..jpg/180px-Looking_into_the_sun_from_over_downtown_onto_the_Homer_Spit..jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Looking_into_the_sun_from_over_downtown_onto_the_Homer_Spit..jpg)

[Homer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homer,_Alaska "Homer, Alaska"), showing (from bottom to top) the edge of downtown, [its airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homer_Airport "Homer Airport") and [the Spit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homer_Spit "Homer Spit")

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/45/Barrow-Alaska-skyview.jpg/180px-Barrow-Alaska-skyview.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Barrow-Alaska-skyview.jpg)

[Utqiaġvik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Utqia%C4%A1vik,_Alaska "Utqiaġvik, Alaska") (Browerville neighborhood near [Eben Hopson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eben_Hopson "Eben Hopson") Middle School shown), known colloquially for many years by the nickname "Top of the World", is the northernmost city in the United States.

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/aa/CordovaHillside.jpg/180px-CordovaHillside.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:CordovaHillside.jpg)

[Cordova](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cordova,_Alaska "Cordova, Alaska"), built in the early 20th century to support the [Kennecott Mines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kennecott_Mines "Kennecott Mines") and the [Copper River and Northwestern Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copper_River_and_Northwestern_Railway "Copper River and Northwestern Railway"), has been preserved as a fishing community since their closure.

[![](https://upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/a1/Downtown_Talkeetna.jpg/180px-Downtown_Talkeetna.jpg)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Downtown_Talkeetna.jpg)

Main Street in [Talkeetna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talkeetna "Talkeetna")

Alaska is not divided into [counties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/County_\(United_States\) "County (United States)"), as most of the other U.S. states, but it is divided into *[boroughs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_boroughs_and_census_areas_in_Alaska "List of boroughs and census areas in Alaska")*.[\[57\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-58) Delegates to the [Alaska Constitutional Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_Constitutional_Convention "Alaska Constitutional Convention") wanted to avoid the pitfalls of the traditional county system and adopted their own unique model.[\[58\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-59) Many of the more densely populated parts of the state are part of Alaska's 16 boroughs, which function somewhat similarly to counties in other states. Unlike county-equivalents in the other states, the boroughs do not cover the state's entire land area. The area not part of any borough is referred to as the [Unorganized Borough](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unorganized_Borough,_Alaska "Unorganized Borough, Alaska").

The Unorganized Borough has no government of its own, but the [U.S. Census Bureau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Census_Bureau "U.S. Census Bureau") in cooperation with the state divided the Unorganized Borough into 11 [census areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Census_area "Census area") solely for the purposes of statistical analysis and presentation. A *recording district* is a mechanism for management of the [public record](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_record "Public record") in Alaska. The state is divided into 34 recording districts which are centrally administered under a state [recorder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recorder_of_deeds "Recorder of deeds"). All recording districts use the same acceptance criteria, fee schedule, etc., for accepting documents into the public record.

Whereas many U.S. states use a three-tiered system of decentralization—state/county/township—most of Alaska uses only two tiers—state/borough. Owing to the low population density, most of the land is located in the [Unorganized Borough](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unorganized_Borough "Unorganized Borough"). As the name implies, it has no intermediate borough government but is administered directly by the state government. In 2000, 57.71% of Alaska's area has this status, with 13.05% of the population.[\[59\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-60)

Anchorage merged the city government with the Greater Anchorage Area Borough in 1975 to form the Municipality of Anchorage, containing the city proper and the communities of Eagle River, Chugiak, Peters Creek, Girdwood, Bird, and Indian. Fairbanks has a separate borough (the [Fairbanks North Star Borough](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fairbanks_North_Star_Borough "Fairbanks North Star Borough")) and municipality (the City of Fairbanks).

The state's most populous city is [Anchorage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anchorage,_Alaska "Anchorage, Alaska"), home to 291,247 people in 2020.[\[60\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-2020_Census_Data-61) The richest [location in Alaska by per capita income](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_locations_by_per_capita_income "Alaska locations by per capita income") is [Denali](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denali_Borough,_Alaska "Denali Borough, Alaska") ($42,245). [Yakutat City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yakutat_City "Yakutat City"), Sitka, Juneau, and Anchorage are the four [largest cities in the U.S. by area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_U.S._cities_by_area "List of U.S. cities by area").

#### Cities and census-designated places (by population)

\[[edit](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Alaska\&action=edit\&section=20 "Edit section: Cities and census-designated places (by population)")]

As reflected in the [2020 United States census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_United_States_census "2020 United States census"), Alaska has a total of 355 incorporated cities and [census-designated places](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Census-designated_place "Census-designated place") (CDPs).[\[61\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-2020_Census_Gazetter-62) The tally of cities includes four unified municipalities, essentially the equivalent of a [consolidated city–county](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consolidated_city%E2%80%93county "Consolidated city–county"). The majority of these communities are located in the rural expanse of Alaska known as "[The Bush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Bush_\(Alaska\) "The Bush (Alaska)")" and are unconnected to that contiguous North American road network. The table at the bottom of this section lists the 100 largest cities and census-designated places in Alaska, in population order.

Of Alaska's 2020 U.S. census population figure of 733,391, 16,655 people, or 2.27% of the population, did not live in an incorporated city or census-designated place.[\[60\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/#cite_note-2020_Census_Data-61) Approximately three-quarters of that figure were people who live in urban and suburban neighborhoods on the outskirts of the city limits of Ketchikan, Kodiak, Palmer and Wasilla. CDPs have not been established for these areas by the United States Census Bureau, except that seven CDPs were established for the Ketchikan-area neighborhoods in the [1980 census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1980_United_States_census "1980 United States census") (Clover Pass, Herring Cove, Ketchikan East, Mountain Point, [Alaska Route 7](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alaska_Route_7 "Alaska Route 7"), [Pennock Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pennock_Island "Pennock Island") and [Saxman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Saxman,_Alaska "Saxman, Alaska") East), but have not been used since. The remaining population was scattered throughout Alaska, both within organized boroughs and in the Unorganized Borough, in largely remote areas.\[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed "Wikipedia:Citation needed")*]
